AVERAGE DAY IN THE LIFE OF {PET}
7am-9am Wake up 
Let {PET} out as soon as you get up, then I go back inside to brush my teeth, have tea, etc. Then I take him for a walk to the park. He will be pulling and zig zagging bc he is SO excited for the park. Then we play fetch for 30-60mins. I let him roam and sniff around while keeping an eye on him. He likes to eat grass, that is fine. There is usually a drinking area where I take him. Anytime after 11am these days is sorta too hot for him. We come home, I wiped his feet etc. if he is dirty then I feed him. Always feed him after exercise not before obvi. 
If im home I let him out every 3-4 hours. If Im out he can hold his bladder for 6-7, just let him out to pee before you leave. 
I take him out for a second park play time between 5-8 depending on my schedule, for 30-60mins. If it is raining, depending on how gross it is and my mood, ill either take him to the park or walk him around the block. Then I feed him an hour or so after his second park moment/walk. 
I also always give him a treat when i put him in the crate. I dont use the crate w him anymore but sometimes he will go in there on his own to chill. 
FOOD/FEEDING:
200g raw food
100g dry food
1-2 pumps of salmon oil
Splash of cold water
I feed him every 12hrs, so roughly like 8am-8pm, 9am-9pm, if I feed him at 10am, ill feed him at 8-9pm ish since I think 10pm to feed is too late. 
BONE/bully stick:
Ill give him one of these every 2 days after his walk in the morning, in the middle of the day or after dinner as like a late night treat. 
Commands he knows
SIT - fist up
DOWN - fist down
PAW - hand/fist out
KISS - stick your nose/mouth out to him
TOUCH - hand down for a “high five”, this is to get him to come to me or get him off the bed etc.
STAY - hand out for a "high five”
NO - just saying it assertively, this is so he stops a behaviour or doesn’t pick up dangerous things that I drop on the ground

{DATE}
Hi {SITTER}! Sending you some notes about {PET}. Please let me know if you have any questions
6:01 p.m.
8:00am Wake Up and go for walk
8:30am Lunch
2:30pm 2nd potty break
6:00pm Dinner
8-9pm 3rd potty break before bed
**3 walks per day + 2 meals per day
Feeding - 
 - Use slow feeder for her bowl
 - Tin bowl is for water
 - Food per meal: 
 - Water (little bit in bowl)
 - 1x 1/3 cup kibble
 - 1x 1/4 teaspoon pumpkin powder
 - 3x 1/4 teaspoon toppers
 - If she doesn’t eat, leave it out and don’t give her more food until she finishes it
 - If she doesn’t finish her dinner, throw out the remaining the next day. Don’t give her the same food that has been left overnight.
 - Before eating: tell her to sit and wait. Say “ok go!” and point at the bowl. Sometimes you have to leave the area for her to eat (leave her alone).
Reactivity - 
- She is reactive to other dogs when on leash. DO NOT allow her to socialize with other dogs on walks. I usually wait with her on the side until the other dog has passed. Ask her to “wait”.
Leaving the house - 
- No need to tell her when you are leaving or coming home.
- When you come home, DO NOT greet her until she calms down.
Training - bring treats on walks!!
- sit when in elevator 
- Wait before exiting the elevator
- Wait+sit before crossing the street 
- Sit + Wait before eating 
Dog parks
- If you would like to take her to the dog park, unleash her before going into the park. Make sure to watch her at all times.
- She will get scared if big dogs if they approach her too fast.
Winter - 
- If it’s snowing or has snow on the ground, please put on her booties. Her paws get cold easily.
Other - 
- {PET} is afraid of text message noises, especially when they are high pitched.

{PET} Instructions
Food:
- He eats about two times a day. I just leave the food out and let him eat whenever. 
- Of course water is out all the time. He sometimes drinks water in the middle of the night so it should be accessible to him.
Walks:
- He needs to walk 3 times a day; Morning, late afternoon and before bed.
- His collar is a martin gale collar. The big loop goes over his head.
- He usually barks and reacts to other dogs. There is a special way to introduce him to other dogs by avoiding pulling the leash, saying "hi hi hi" and walking towards the dog. I only do this with small dogs but I suggest you avoid other dogs if you are not comfortable.
- Make sure to hold the leash tight as he will run to another dog if he sees one. He doesn't care if there are cars.
- He hates the rain so in case it rains, I packed a rain jacket for him.
- He doesn't like men in the elevator/lobby/street so I keep him on a short leash if there are people around just in case. He might attack men's ankles.
Leaving at home:
- He is ok to stay by himself for up to 8 hours. 
- He sometimes has separation anxiety and may pee if left alone. I usually walk him right before I leave him and then put a pee pad in case he gets scared and has an accident.
Sleep:
- He is good at sleeping in his bed and likes to be covered completely under the covers.
- He may try to jump in your bed and cuddle in the early morning.
Treats:
- You can give him some treats but make him sit or paw before giving.
Commands he knows:
sit, stay, off, leave it, drop it, twirl, let's go

6:10 p.m.
Thank you {SITTER}! {PET} is excited for her stay. I'm planning to come at 8am on Monday to drop her off if that still works. 
Also, here are the instructions for her care that I promised. It should all be straightforward, but let me know if you have any questions.
Caring for {PET} ({PET})
Thank you so much for taking care of {PET} while we are gone. We hope you have an amazing time with her. We think she’s pretty great.
Here are some tips for caring for {PET}, but we are also open to you finding your own rhythm and going with your instincts. Hopefully you will be able to give us some tips on what worked for you that we have never thought of!
1.Eating
{PET} gets 2 meals a day of ½ cup of dogfood. Usually the first serving is given between 7am-8am, second between 6pm-7pm. When you feed her, please replace her water too.
She is a stubborn eater (she will sometimes refuse food if she believes she can hold out for treats) and she has a very sensitive stomach and some days doesn’t feel up to eating if she has previously eaten something she shouldn’t. So don’t worry if she skips a meal or even potentially if she skips a whole day. (We usually only worry if she has skipped two full days in a row).
I’ve given you some treats, but the raw hides in particular upset her stomach if she has too many. We usually don’t give her more than 1/week.
2.Walks
{PET} walks best using the chest clip on her 3-point harness and not great if you clip the leash to her collar or back instead. The 3-point harness is a bit tricky to figure out which way it goes. I suggest leaving her harness on the whole time she is with you (i.e. even at night) so you don’t have to worry about getting it on and off.
She generally goes on a walk in the morning around 8 / 8:30am for 45 minutes minimum, and often a second walk around dinner. We then just go out for a pee before bed. She also gets a couple of pee breaks during the day. 
She will poop regularly in the morning and usually a second time around the dinner walk. Though sometimes she will poop twice in the morning and that’s it!
 
3.Play
{PET} doesn’t love toys. She will chase a ball but might get very possessive. She loves cuddles and particularly tummy rubs.
4.Bedtime
{PET} is very good at night as long as she is not by an open window with sudden noise. She will bark if she thinks a person is walking by her (which happens sometimes with an open window and sudden talking or loud walking). Otherwise, she will be very quiet until you wake up. It’s ideal if she sleeps in her crate (so that she doesn’t wander and bark out a window) but she is also good sleeping on the floor in any room with a closed door. She will do best sleeping in the room you are in. Usually we bring her into our room with the door closed and with her crate door open. When we turn off the lights and get in bed, she generally gets bored and goes into her crate and we close the door. Or sometimes we don’t close the door and she naturally sleeps there all night, sometimes moving to the carpet by our bed mid evening.
5.Staying home alone
{PET} can stay home alone, but she gets anxious in new places and will sometimes chew something up (e.g. a shoe or a bag) or have an accident. If you do leave her alone for a short time, I would give her the raw hide while you are out.
6.Barking/Aggression
While sweet and loving, she is territorial by instinct and vocal when she gets anxious. She will bark whenever she is surprised which can include someone knocking on the door or walking by the door. Out in the world, she will bark at a dog or a person if she senses danger.
Keep her on a tight leash when walking by people or dogs – she can surprise you and lunge at someone if you give her too much rope when passing others.
7.Off-leash
I would suggest avoiding letting her off-leash outside the house. She is unpredictable and can run off or attack people/dogs if you don’t know her signals.
In summary, she is a very loyal, loving dog. Take her walks, give her tummy rubs and feed her and she will adore you! Just be cautious of her anxiety in the wider world, especially when something scares her. 
Thank you for taking such good care of {PET}! Don’t hesitate to give us a call if you need anything:
{OWNER} – {PHONE}

1. Food/Feeding schedule
{PET} eats twice a day after his walks. One scoop of dry food goes in his snuffle mat or another toy.
He can have two snacks a day, around 12 pm and 4 pm. These could be a couple of cookies.
The starting point for feeding and snacking is his bed. I usually hide the cookies around the house, and he needs to find them.
2. Walking
{PET} must walk twice a day, and on weekends, he has an extra walk. If you want to walk him more than twice, I know he will be happy, but the third walk is on you. Walks take 20 to 30 minutes. He usually poops twice during each walk.
During walks, I work on preventing him from pulling the leash by tapping it and calling his attention with a sound. Regarding other dogs, he is improving with males but can sometimes become aggressive with them. No problem with female dogs—he loves them!
3. Commands/tricks
{PET} loves to be petted, but I recommend limiting his cuddles and allowing him to stay calm in his bed. He sleeps on my bed at the end of the bed. He is very well-behaved during the night, but since this is his first time in your place, he might stay alert all the time.
Here are some Spanish words I use with {PET}:
"A tú cama": go to your bed.
"Ahora": right now (with a snap of your fingers), the signal to eat.
"Busca": look for it (snack time).
"Sentado": sit down.
4. Habits we should know
{PET} is a good boy, but as we discussed before, he barks. Although he is improving with training, he still tends to talk a lot. You can always correct this with a firm "NO" or "Shuu." I'm sure your dog experience will be helpful for {PET}.
Something that could be a bit tricky before going for a walk is the leash. That is something that we are also working on with the trainer. He tries to hide and barks when I take the leash, so the first step is to call him, ask him to sit, and then take the leash.
